- `B005-F06-007`: — <code>symbol_name_index</code> schema (lines 100-107): primary key <code>(shard_key, symbol_name)</code>; store <code>shard_key</code>, <code>symbol_name</code>, and MessagePack <code>Vec&lt;SymbolRef {file,id}&gt;</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:257)_
- `B005-F06-008`: — <code>symbol_reverse_index</code> schema (lines 108-113): primary key <code>(shard_bucket, callee_file)</code>; bucket is 0–255 from the first SHA1 byte of <code>callee_file</code>; payload is MessagePack caller-file strings. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:258)_
- `B005-F06-009`: — <code>symbol_file_payload</code> schema (lines 114-120): file is primary key; persist language, blake3 content hash, MessagePack symbols, and MessagePack edges. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:259)_
- `B005-F06-010`: — <code>symbol_graph_meta</code> schema (lines 121-131): key by project ID and store symbol/edge/file counts, unresolved-edge percentage, injected-clock <code>built_at</code>, and schema version; migration is v3 and adds no SQLite dependency. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:260)_
- `B005-F06-011`: — symbol domain types (lines 133-140): <code>SymbolKind</code> values are Function, Method, Class, Struct, Trait, Module, Enum, Interface, Type; <code>SymbolNode</code>, <code>SymbolEdge</code>, <code>SymbolRef</code>, and <code>EntryPoint</code> have the exact fields stated, and all serialize through serde/MessagePack. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:261)_
- `B005-F06-012`: — confidence model (lines 136-138): <code>Confidence</code> is exactly <code>Local|Unique|MultipleCandidates|Unresolved</code>; each edge also carries candidate IDs and a <code>CallSite</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:262)_
- `B005-F06-013`: — sharding functions (lines 142-145): <code>name_shard_key</code> returns the first lowercase ASCII letter or underscore; <code>reverse_shard_bucket</code> returns first SHA1 byte of file; <code>all_name_shard_keys()</code> returns all 27 keys. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:263)_
- `B005-F06-014`: — extraction contract (lines 147-155): <code>extract_symbols_and_calls(source, language, file)</code> uses existing tree-sitter grammars, not ast-grep; raw calls contain caller ID, callee name, and call site; every file gets a synthetic <code>&lt;module&gt;</code> caller for unattributed calls. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:264)_
- `B005-F06-015`: — resolver tiers (lines 156-165): resolution searches same-file Local, one-hop Imported, then two-hop Re-export dependencies; zero candidates yields Unresolved, one Unique, and multiple MultipleCandidates; mutate <code>outgoing_calls_by_file</code> in place. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:265)_
- `B005-F06-016`: — eager metadata/lazy indexes (lines 166-177): <code>SymbolGraphCache</code> eagerly holds tiny metadata; first name query loads 27 shards in parallel through <code>tokio::JoinSet</code>; first impact query loads 256 reverse shards in parallel; file payloads are lazy. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:266)_
- `B005-F06-017`: — LRU implementation/capacity (lines 176-181): cache at most 500 <code>Arc&lt;SymbolGraphFilePayload&gt;</code> entries; implement a tiny LRU with existing/standard facilities and obtain explicit confirmation before any new dependency; constructor accepts project ID, SQLite pool, and configurable capacity. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:267)_
- `B005-F06-018`: — impact traversal API (lines 183-192): <code>compute_impact_radius(cache,target,depth)</code> recognizes file path versus symbol name, BFSes the reverse-file index, returns deterministic <code>BTreeMap&lt;u32,Vec&lt;String&gt;&gt;</code> hop bands plus <code>truncated</code>, and clamps depth to ten. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:268)_
- `B005-F06-019`: — call-flow API (lines 193-198): <code>compute_call_flow</code> performs cycle-aware DFS, loads child payloads only while descending, clamps depth to ten, and annotates leaves with <code>TruncationReason::Depth|Cycle</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:269)_
- `B005-F06-020`: — symbol-context API (lines 199-202): <code>compute_symbol_context(cache,name,file_hint)</code> returns every matching definition, not only the first, with callers/callees and confidence; file hint disambiguates. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:270)_
- `B005-F06-021`: — symbol-list API (lines 203-204): <code>list_symbols</code> filters by optional file, optional query, and required limit. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:271)_
- `B005-F06-022`: — invalidation boundary (lines 206-210): <code>invalidate_file(file)</code> evicts its payload and marks name/reverse indexes for partial rebuild; more than fifty changed files forces a full rebuild. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:272)_
- `B005-F06-023`: — full build lifecycle (lines 212-223): after DKG build, extract each file, resolve against the import graph, build deterministic shard maps, persist all four table families as MessagePack, and log files/symbols/edges/unresolved percentage. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:273)_
- `B005-F06-024`: — incremental build lifecycle (lines 225-229): when changed count is at most <code>INCREMENTAL_SYMBOL_THRESHOLD</code>, patch changed/removed payloads and only affected name/reverse buckets; otherwise rebuild fully. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:274)_
- `B005-F06-025`: — dispatcher schemas (lines 231-243): register <code>symbol_impact {target,depth?}</code>, <code>symbol_flow {entrypoint?,file?,depth?}</code>, <code>symbol_context {name,file?}</code>, and <code>symbol_list {file?,query?,limit?}</code>; flow without an entrypoint lists B-34 candidates if available, otherwise errors. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:275)_
- `B005-F06-026`: — Auditor auto-injection (lines 245-250): in PostToolUse Write/Edit, for every changed file call impact at depth two; if any files are impacted, append <code>IMPACT_SUMMARY</code> to the Auditor briefing. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:276)_
- `B005-F06-027`: — query event schema (lines 252-254): append <code>symbol_query</code> per query with <code>{tool,target,hop_count,total_files,latency_ms}</code> for dashboard activity. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:277)_
- `B005-F06-028`: — dashboard impact panel (lines 256-260): KnowledgeDrawer shows unresolved-edge percentage, last target/depth/total/hop table, and a <code>/symbol_impact</code> input routed through synthetic <code>submit_task</code> intent. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:278)_
- `B005-F06-029`: — produced symbol registry (lines 262-278): export cache/traversal/resolution/extraction/sharding functions, <code>SsbActionType::SymbolQuery</code>, and IPC constants <code>TOOL_SYMBOL_IMPACT</code>, <code>TOOL_SYMBOL_FLOW</code>, <code>TOOL_SYMBOL_CONTEXT</code>, <code>TOOL_SYMBOL_LIST</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:279)_
- `B005-F06-030`: — integration registry (lines 279-293): full/update indexing call graph builds; TaskRunner PostToolUse performs depth-two impact; dashboard <code>get_impact_analysis</code> reads meta/hops; CLI research uses symbol context; paths are reachable from CLI, dashboard, and TaskRunner. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:280)_
- `B005-F06-031`: — cache/data stub bans (lines 294-300): first impact query must really load reverse index when metadata reports edges; LRU capacity must be 500; ELAI’s unresolved-edge percentage must be below 50%, with below 30% ideal but not mandatory. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:281)_
- `B005-F06-032`: — frontend dependency (lines 301-302): B-33 is the frontend pair and consumes the same cache for symbol-mode visualization. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:282)_
- `B005-F06-033`: — observable contract (lines 303-307): the four-tool dispatch smoke writes one <code>symbol_query</code> row per tool and stdout must contain a real “Hop 1” block with ELAI paths. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:283)_
- `B005-F06-034`: — database acceptance bounds (lines 309-325): all four tables must exist; ELAI meta requires at least 5,000 symbols, 8,000 edges, and under 50% unresolved; reverse index must contain a known multi-caller clock entry. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:284)_
- `B005-F06-035`: — CLI behavioral checks (lines 326-341): impact on <code>ClockProvider</code> should return depth three and at least twenty files; flow from CLI <code>main</code> should show a real call tree; an edit rename task must yield an Auditor <code>IMPACT_SUMMARY</code> with at least five dependent files. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:285)_
- `B005-F06-036`: — boundedness check (lines 343-345): after one thousand payload accesses, LRU size remains at most 500, covered by <code>test_lru_capacity_bounded</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:286)_
- `B005-F06-037`: — acceptance gates (lines 348-366): property-test depth/cycles/target polymorphism, persist 27/256 shards, make v3 idempotent, integrate full/incremental indexing, expose all four tools through CLI/dashboard/IPC, inject impact, emit latency telemetry, render real UI, use no non-test unwrap, and add no unconfirmed dependency. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:287)_
- `B005-F06-038`: — extraction/resolution tests (lines 368-375): fixtures cover Rust/Python/TS/Go/JVM counts/names and Local/Imported/Re-export call sites with asserted confidence. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:288)_
- `B005-F06-039`: — cache/traversal tests (lines 376-383): test capacity, parallel shard loading, payload eviction/index preservation, depth bounds, cycle termination, all-match context, and list limits. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:289)_
- `B005-F06-040`: — end-to-end/contracts (lines 384-387): add IPC <code>symbol_query_dispatch.rs</code> and <code>tests/contracts/symbol_graph_contract.rs</code> for meta/schema sanity. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:290)_
- `B005-F06-041`: — implementation footprint (lines 389-412): create the complete <code>knowledge::symbol_graph</code> module set, store and migration; modify knowledge export, indexing, dispatcher, TaskRunner, SSB action types, V2 Tauri, KnowledgeDrawer; create <code>B-29-decisions.md</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:291)_
- `B005-F06-042`: — required decisions (lines 414-427): document SQLite MessagePack shards, std-only LRU, in-place confidence mutation, always-on versus feature-gated <code>IMPACT_SUMMARY</code> (recommend always-on with env opt-out), default/user depth, and FocalAD as the design anchor. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:292)_
- `B005-F06-043`: — unlocked capabilities (lines 429-442): provide evidence-based edit scope, existing-project call-flow grounding, authoritative Oracle caller answers, B-23 blast-radius signals, and zero-extra-load B-33/B-34 reuse. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:293)_
- `B005-F06-044`: — downstream (lines 444-448): B-30 is independent; B-33 waits for the cache; B-34 waits for file payloads. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:294)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0214`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Four agent-callable operations use a sharded, LRU-bounded `SymbolGraphCache`: `compute_impact_radius` does reverse-call BFS with hop bands and depth <=10; `compute_call_flow` does cycle-aware DFS with lazy children and depth <=10; `compute_symbol_context` returns definition plus confidence-tagged callers/callees; `list_symbols` filters by file/query/limit. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:373)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0215`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Cache layout: 27 name shards (`a-z` plus `_`), 256 reverse shards keyed by single-byte SHA1 prefix, and a 500-file payload LRU. A query must never load every symbol or edge. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:374)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0216`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Resolver confidence is one of `Local`, `Imported` (one hop), `Re-export` (two hops), or `Unresolved`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:375)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0217`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` DKG schema v3 tables are `symbol_name_index`, `symbol_reverse_index`, `symbol_file_payload`, and `symbol_graph_meta`, with MessagePack BLOBs. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:376)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0218`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Dispatcher entries are `symbol_impact`, `symbol_flow`, `symbol_context`, `symbol_list`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:377)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0219`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Auditor `PostToolUse` auto-injects `IMPACT_SUMMARY` at depth two for changed files. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:378)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0220`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Fewer than fifty changed files triggers incremental invalidation; more than fifty triggers full rebuild. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:379)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0221`: section=`B-29 - Symbol-level impact analysis API - PLANNED; highest-leverage recommendation` Implement LRU using std only, with no `lru` crate. FocalAD (`arXiv:2506.11419`) is the stated research anchor for top-k local focus. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:380)_

### B-29.5: Symbol-graph scale benchmark (release gate)
- **Status:** planned
- **Anchors:** `B29S-001` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:229; `B29S-002` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:230; `B29S-003` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:231; `B29S-004` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:232; `B29S-005` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:233; `B29S-006` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:234; `B29S-007` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:235; `B29S-008` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:236; `B29S-009` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:237; `B29S-010` @ DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:238
- **Composes with:**
  - `B-004/X-E06` ↔ B-25, B-31.5: B-31.5 reuses B-29.5’s exact fixtures, can run in parallel, and explicitly turns B-25’s Empire-corpus claim into a testable scale assertion (B-31.5 L10-L13, L475-L499).
  - `B-006/INT-013` ↔ B-29, B-31, B-31.5: : B-29.5 is an evidence gate over B-29 traversal/cache claims and depends on B-31 resume; B-31.5 shares fixtures but measures an orthogonal indexing domain (B29.5 lines 1-12, 192-209, 458-461).
  - `B-006/INT-020` ↔ B-29, B-30, B-35: : B-29.5’s per-language unresolved-edge evidence can prioritize B-30 extractor work and can improve the B-29 symbol context paired with B-35, but neither benchmark completion nor artifact completion directly implements the other (B29.5 line
  - `B29S-002` → B-29, B-31, B-31.5: Dependencies/parallelism: B-29 and resumable B-31 are prerequisites; B-31.5 may run in parallel and share fixtures (lines 8-12).
  - `B29S-019` → B-31: Recovery dependency: Linux/Chromium indexing is expected to take one to three hours and must use B-31 resume after interruption (lines 207-209, 324-329).
- `B29S-001`: Scope: The benchmark must prove B-29’s cache and four traversals at Linux-kernel, Chromium, VS Code, and PostgreSQL scale without OOM, with p99 below 500ms and stable hop-banded output (lines 1-7). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:229)_
- `B29S-002`: Dependencies/parallelism: B-29 and resumable B-31 are prerequisites; B-31.5 may run in parallel and share fixtures (lines 8-12). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:230)_
- `B29S-003`: Risk thesis: Sharding, LRU payload bounds, hop-banded BFS, and lazy DFS were only proven on about 140K LOC/8,075 nodes; the benchmark must establish that no query loads the full graph and memory follows result-set size rather than total graph size (lines 14-37). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:231)_
- `B29S-004`: External-evidence boundary: SocratiCode’s 40M-LOC claim is treated as unverified because its located test is about 200K symbols/10K files; ELAI must generate its own evidence (lines 24-27). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:232)_
- `B29S-005`: Research anchor: FocalAD ELAI arXiv:2506.11419 supports top-k local-focus selection, while this benchmark is explicitly the engineering bounded-memory/latency proof (lines 39-45). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:233)_
- `B29S-006`: Fixture root: Fixtures live outside .elai_cc at configurable ELAI_SCALE_FIXTURE_ROOT, defaulting to a home-directory elai_scale_fixtures path; this prevents ordinary project indexing from ingesting them (lines 64-78). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:234)_
- `B29S-007`: Linux fixture: clone_scale_fixtures.sh shallow-clones torvalds/linux v6.7, approximately 36M LOC and primarily C/asm (lines 79-81, 99-104). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:235)_
- `B29S-008`: Chromium fixture: The script shallow-clones chromium/src 121.0.6167.184 without depot_tools, approximately 35M LOC and mixed C++/JS/TS/Python/ObjC (lines 82-85, 105-110). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:236)_
- `B29S-009`: VS Code fixture: The script shallow-clones microsoft/vscode 1.94.2, approximately 2.45M LOC of TS/JS/CSS/HTML (lines 86-88, 111-116). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:237)_
- `B29S-010`: PostgreSQL fixture: The script shallow-clones postgres REL_17_0, approximately 1.5M LOC of C/Perl/Tcl/SQL/shell (lines 89-91, 117-122). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:238)_
- `B29S-011`: Reproducible acquisition: Fixture tags/commits are pinned, existing directories are not recloned, and phase_b29_5_fixture_inventory.toml records paths, pins, exact measured LOC, and languages to prevent HEAD drift (lines 70-122). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:239)_
- `B29S-012`: Criterion harness: symbol_graph_scale.rs benchmarks impact_radius, call_flow, symbol_context, and list_symbols separately per tier/tool with criterion sample_size 20 and fixed high-fan-in symbols (lines 124-165). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:240)_
- `B29S-013`: Per-language diagnosis: Separating each tier/tool combination exposes degradation such as template-heavy C++ resolution versus plain C (lines 163-165). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:241)_
- `B29S-014`: Memory snapshot schema: MemorySnapshot records rss_bytes, heap_bytes, cache_lru_size, name_index_loaded, and reverse_index_loaded (lines 167-178). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:242)_
- `B29S-015`: Memory instrumentation: RSS uses procfs on Linux and GetProcessMemoryInfo on Windows. Heap may use jemalloc-ctl only if jemalloc is already global; adding it requires user approval (lines 179-181). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:243)_
- `B29S-016`: Realistic workload: The workload is exactly 1,000 queries: 60% impact_radius depth 2-3, 25% symbol_context, 10% call_flow, and 5% list_symbols, starting from a cold-loaded cache (lines 183-190). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:244)_
- `B29S-017`: Workload metrics: Each tool records p50/p95/p99, plus LRU hit rate, peak memory, and final file_payload_lru size, which must be <= 500 (lines 189-190, 219-229). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:245)_
- `B29S-018`: Indexing evidence: All four fixtures are indexed with per-tier metrics covering wall time, peak indexing RSS, node/edge counts per language, symbol_count, edge_count, file_count, and unresolved_edge_pct (lines 192-209). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:246)_
- `B29S-019`: Recovery dependency: Linux/Chromium indexing is expected to take one to three hours and must use B-31 resume after interruption (lines 207-209, 324-329). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:247)_
- `B29S-020`: Benchmark artifact: Criterion JSON is written to phase_b29_5_bench_raw.json, expected valid and over 100KB with tier × tool entries (lines 211-218, 338-341). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:248)_
- `B29S-021`: Repeat determinism: The bench runs twice; a target must return identical total_files and identical hop-band sets, with cross-run variance below 10% for the reported p99 observation (lines 216-218, 305-310). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:249)_
- `B29S-022`: Aggregate schema: phase_b29_5_scale.toml records per-tier index counts/timing/shard sizes and nested workload query count, hit rate, peak RSS, and per-tool quantiles (lines 231-256). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:250)_
- `B29S-023`: Five-run regression proof: symbol_graph_determinism runs impact_radius five times and requires byte-identical BTreeMap<u32, Vec<String>> plus identical truncated flags for linux::kmalloc, vscode::vscode_extension, chromium::BrowserMain, and postgres::PortalRunMulti (lines 258-267). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:251)_
- `B29S-024`: CI performance guard: scale_p99_budget_contract reads populated metrics, requires every tool/tier p99 <= 500ms, LRU memory <= 2GB on a 16GB-equivalent machine, and unresolved_edge_pct <= 50% (lines 268-273). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:252)_
- `B29S-025`: Reproduction record: phase_b29_5_README documents exact pins, sample size, target CPU/RAM/storage, and expected per-tier wall time (lines 275-280). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:253)_
- `B29S-026`: Wiring contract: Produced symbols are the criterion harness, MemorySnapshot/snapshot, run_realistic_workload, determinism regression, and p99 CI contract; they consume SymbolGraphCache, all four B-29 traversals, and B-29/B-31 indexing (lines 282-295). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:254)_
- `B29S-027`: Reachability/resource gate: The bench is reached by cargo bench; scale tests are include-ignored because fixtures must be pre-cloned. The CI contract must read populated data and fixture cloning must use pins (lines 295-304). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:255)_
- `B29S-028`: OS-visible observation: A completed run must populate scale metrics and show stable p99 across two runs with <10% variance (lines 305-310). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:256)_
- `B29S-029`: Deterministic done gates: Checks cover pinned HEADs, four resumable indices, nonempty symbol_graph_meta, JSON criterion output, the 500ms contract, five-run hop-band identity, and <=2048MB Chromium workload RSS (lines 312-355). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:257)_
- `B29S-030`: Acceptance performance bounds: Every traversal on every tier must meet p99 <=500ms; a 1,000-query workload must remain <=2GB peak RSS; Linux should have about 5M+ DKG nodes/2M+ symbols and overall unresolved edges below 50% (lines 357-375). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:258)_
- `B29S-031`: Language-quality evidence: Per-language unresolved_edge_pct is documented but not gated because C/C++ dynamic dispatch/macros are expected to be worse than TS/JS (lines 376-378). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:259)_
- `B29S-032`: Failure escalation: Any tier that misses p99 reopens B-29 and requires a decision record; failure may not be silently accepted (lines 379-380). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:260)_
- `B29S-033`: Dependency permission: No external crate, including jemalloc/procfs candidates, may be added without explicit user confirmation (lines 381-382). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:261)_
- `B29S-034`: Required artifacts: The source enumerates the clone script, three bench/workload modules, two tests, fixture/scale/readme/per-tier metrics, and B-29.5-decisions (lines 384-410). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:262)_
- `B29S-035`: Official-run policy: The decision record must choose official hardware, preserve host-specific sibling metrics rather than overwrite official numbers, and decide full-four-tier gating versus the recommended Linux+VS Code minimum (lines 412-423). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:263)_
- `B29S-036`: Budget/scheduling policy: The decision record must justify 500ms, document user-facing hook cost, choose memory instrumentation, and schedule the multi-hour benchmark nightly rather than per commit (lines 424-435). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:264)_
- `B29S-037`: Secondary outcomes: Published metrics ground performance claims, identify weak B-30 extractors, share fixtures with B-31.5/B-30, and expose C++ extraction regressions (lines 437-449). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:265)_
- `B29S-038`: Explicit non-goal: Auditor signal-to-noise for impact results is not measured here; it is only a future self-modification signal candidate (lines 450-456). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:266)_
- `B29S-039`: Lifecycle: B-31.5 uses the same fixtures for an orthogonal metric domain and may run in parallel (lines 458-461). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:267)_
- `B29S-040`: Fixture database path: The symbol-graph check uses {fixture}/.elai_cc/.elai_cc/indexes/dkg.db (lines 331-335); the duplicated directory segment is not explained and must be reconciled with the actual B-1/B-31 layout before automation. _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:268)_
- `B29S-041`: Threshold boundary: The objective says p99 “under 500ms,” while contracts and acceptance use <= 500ms (lines 5, 270, 345, 366). The exact equality boundary is internally inconsistent. _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:269)_
- `B29S-042`: Status: No shipped overlay exists and all acceptance rows are unchecked, so this source is a planned benchmark/release-evidence gate rather than measured proof (lines 357-382). _(anchor: DEEP-B-006.discovery.md:270)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0222`: section=`B-29.5 - Symbol-graph scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Pinned fixtures: Linux v6.7 (~36M LOC), Chromium 121.0.6167.184 (~35M), VS Code 1.94.2 (~2.45M), PostgreSQL `REL_17_0` (~1.5M). _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:386)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0223`: section=`B-29.5 - Symbol-graph scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Acceptance: traversal-tool p99 <=500ms; LRU peak RSS <=2GB over 1,000 queries; byte-identical BTreeMap hop-band output across five runs; document unresolved-edge percentage per language. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:387)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0224`: section=`B-29.5 - Symbol-graph scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Criterion harness includes `scale_memory.rs` using procfs on Linux or `GetProcessMemoryInfo` on Windows and `workload.rs` weighted 60% impact radius, 25% symbol context, 10% call flow, 5% list symbols. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:388)_

- `B31-006`: Ported design combines 50-file content-hash checkpoints, atomic project lock with PID/liveness/staleness, and startup resume/watcher catch-up with a 60-second shutdown deadline (lines 40-63). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:229)_
- `B31-007`: Existing <code>mcp_session_claim</code> remains the FSM/session ownership layer; new locks sit below it at indexing/watcher service scope (lines 65-68). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:230)_
- `B31-008`: Metadata table fields are <code>project_id</code> PK, <code>project_path</code>, <code>indexed_files=0</code>, <code>total_files=0</code>, <code>indexing_status</code>, <code>last_checkpoint_at</code>, <code>batch_size=50</code>, <code>batches_processed=0</code>, optional <code>batches_total</code>, and <code>schema_version=1</code> default (lines 93-109). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:231)_
- `B31-009`: Status domain is exactly <code>in-progress | completed | cancelled</code> and must never be absent at runtime (lines 103; 290-292). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:232)_
- `B31-010`: <code>file_hashes</code> stores project ID, relative path, blake3 content hash, and last-indexed timestamp under composite PK <code>(project_id, relative_path)</code> (lines 110-118). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:233)_
- `B31-011`: The migration is named “v2” although the sample table’s internal <code>schema_version</code> default is 1; the source does not explain that numbering distinction (lines 108-119). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:234)_
- `B31-012`: <code>ProjectMetadataStore</code> methods are <code>load</code>, idempotent <code>save</code>, <code>update_status</code>, <code>get_file_hashes</code> returning a BTreeMap, <code>set_file_hash</code>, <code>delete_file_hash</code>, and cascading <code>delete_project</code> (lines 120-129). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:235)_
- `B31-013`: Build-index Phase A scans indexable files; Phase B hashes content with blake3, loads saved hashes, and skips unchanged files in resume mode (lines 130-137). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:236)_
- `B31-014`: Phase C processes changed/new files in batches of 50; each batch embeds chunks, upserts BM25/vector/DKG, updates file hashes/counters/time, and persists <code>in-progress</code> (lines 138-145). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:237)_
- `B31-015`: Phase D marks <code>completed</code> only after all batches finish (line 145). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:238)_
- `B31-016`: Cancellation is checked at every batch boundary; it persists <code>cancelled</code> and returns <code>IndexResult::Cancelled { batches_completed }</code> (lines 146-149). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:239)_
- `B31-017`: Incremental update reuses batching only for changed hashes and deletes hashes/chunks for files missing from the latest scan (lines 151-155). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:240)_
- `B31-018`: <code>cancel_indexing(project_id) → bool</code> sets a flag for the next boundary and is exposed both as an orchestrator tool and a Tauri command (lines 156-159). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:241)_
- `B31-019`: One-time <code>migrateAbsolutePathKeys</code> strips old project-root prefixes from file-hash keys and is idempotent/safe twice (lines 161-165). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:242)_
- `B31-020`: Lock primitive choice is conditional: use <code>fs2</code> only if already present; otherwise propose hand-rolled <code>O_EXCL + PID</code> and ask before adding a dependency. The shipped header does not state which branch landed (lines 167-175). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:243)_
- `B31-021`: <code>IndexLock::acquire(project_root, LockScope)</code> returns <code>IndexLockGuard</code>; <code>LockScope</code> is <code>Index</code> or <code>Watch</code> (lines 177-180). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:244)_
- `B31-022`: Lock files are <code>&lt;project&gt;/.elai_cc/.index.lock</code> or <code>.watch.lock</code> and contain the holder PID (lines 181-182). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:245)_
- `B31-023`: PID liveness is <code>kill(pid, 0)</code>/SIG-0 semantics on POSIX and <code>OpenProcess</code> on Windows; a dead PID is reclaimable (lines 183-184). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:246)_
- `B31-024`: Holder refreshes mtime every 30 seconds; stale threshold is two minutes (lines 185-186). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:247)_
- `B31-025`: <code>IndexLockGuard</code> is RAII and releases on Drop; the fs2 branch uses <code>try_lock_exclusive</code> (lines 187-188). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:248)_
- `B31-026`: Context description mentions stale reclaim with SIGTERM/<code>taskkill /F</code>, while implementation tasks describe liveness check and reclaim without requiring process termination; do not infer that the shipped lock kills holders (lines 48-53 versus 177-188). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:249)_
- `B31-027`: <code>build_index</code>/<code>update_index</code> acquire Index scope; contention logs “Another process is already indexing this project, skipping” and returns <code>AlreadyLocked { holder_pid }</code>; RAII releases on both success and error (lines 190-195). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:250)_
- `B31-028`: Watch scope permits multiple instances to observe, but only one actively patches; secondaries report “active (watched by another process)” (lines 196-199). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:251)_
- `B31-029`: Diagnostic <code>IndexLock::is_locked_by_other(project_root, scope) → Option&lt;u32&gt;</code> reports foreign holder PID (lines 201-204). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:252)_
- `B31-030`: <code>auto_resume_indexed_projects()</code> is invoked in CLI after lock initialization/before dispatch and in dashboard after Tauri setup/before its first window (lines 206-214). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:253)_
- `B31-031`: Startup discovers every project with metadata, supporting multi-project deployments (lines 215-217). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:254)_
- `B31-032`: For <code>in-progress</code>, startup tries Index scope and calls <code>build_index(..., resume=true)</code>; if another live PID owns it, startup logs and skips (lines 218-223). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:255)_
- `B31-033`: For <code>completed</code>, startup acquires Watch scope and immediately runs incremental catch-up (lines 224-227). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:256)_
- `B31-034`: For <code>cancelled</code>, startup does nothing; only an explicit user re-index restarts it (lines 228-229). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:257)_
- `B31-035`: Each project resumes in a fire-and-forget Tokio task; failures are logged and never fail startup (lines 230-231). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:258)_
- `B31-036`: <code>graceful_shutdown</code> accepts SIGINT, SIGTERM, or dashboard stdin EOF, sets global <code>shutting_down</code>, and prevents new work (lines 233-237). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:259)_
- `B31-037`: Shutdown cancels every active index and stops all watchers/releasing Watch locks (lines 238-240). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:260)_
- `B31-038`: Shutdown waits at most 60 seconds for batch checkpoint and lock release, then force-releases lock files and logs timestamp/PID (lines 241-247). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:261)_
- `B31-039`: CLI signal handler and Tauri exit handler must both reach graceful shutdown (lines 246-247). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:262)_
- `B31-040`: <code>IndexCheckpoint</code> fields are project ID, processed/total batches, processed files, and last-checkpoint time; it emits every batch (lines 249-251). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:263)_
- `B31-041`: <code>IndexResume</code> fields are project ID, resumed batch, and files skipped; it emits once per resume (line 251). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:264)_
- `B31-042`: <code>IndexCancel</code> fields are project ID, completed batches, and indexed files; lock observability adds <code>LockAcquired</code>/<code>LockContested</code> (lines 252-253). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:265)_
- `B31-043`: Dashboard progress panel replaces static progress with live checkpoints and shows exact resume and lock-contention/read-only banners including batch/file savings and holder PID (lines 255-261). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:266)_
- `B31-044`: Produced contract includes all metadata methods, lock types, startup functions, five SSB variants, cancellation, and <code>IndexResult::{Cancelled, AlreadyLocked, Resumed}</code> (lines 263-274). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:267)_
- `B31-045`: Consumers/reachability cover CLI start/signal, dashboard run/exit, dashboard cancellation, watcher Watch scope, and both indexing writers’ Index scope (lines 275-289). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:268)_
- `B31-046`: Stub bans require a valid status, Drop release, a 60-second nonzero deadline, and startup registration from both CLI and dashboard (lines 290-296). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:269)_
- `B31-047`: Crash contract kills an index, restarts it, requires <code>IndexResume</code> from a nonzero batch, byte/ID-equivalent chunks with no duplicates, and released lock (lines 298-303). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:270)_
- `B31-048`: Deterministic evidence checks schema tables, completed counters, crash-resume node count, concurrent rejection with PID, auto-resume within five seconds, process exit within 65 seconds, and lock-file cleanup (lines 305-349). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:271)_
- `B31-049`: Acceptance requires automatic migration, 50-file checkpointing, boundary cancellation, blake3 skip, lock coverage, PID-visible contention, both startup calls, signal/exit shutdown, five visible SSB events, duplicate-free crash identity, path-key migration, and no unconfirmed dependency (lines 351-375). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:272)_
- `B31-050`: Test suite covers metadata CRUD/migration idempotence, kill/resume identity, contention/stale reclaim/Drop, startup resume, shutdown/cancel SSB, and a type-level lock-required resilience contract (lines 376-392). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:273)_
- `B31-051`: File lifecycle creates metadata SQL/module, lock/startup modules, panel, and decisions; modifies indexing service, CLI lifecycle, V2 Tauri run/exit/cancel, and five action types (lines 394-418). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:274)_
- `B31-052`: Decision doc retains choices for primitive, fixed/adaptive batches, 2m/5m stale timeout, blake3/sha256, session-claim interaction, 60s/120s shutdown, and immediate/natural stale release even though the header says decisions and implementation already exist (lines 420-439). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:275)_
- `B31-053`: Explicit side benefits are large-repo resilience, B-25 readiness, live progress, incremental B-29 graph builds, sane user cancellation, cross-session corruption prevention, and restart without manual <code>/index</code> (lines 441-456). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:276)_
- `B31-054`: B-35 depends on B-31 lock primitives; B-32 touches the same indexer; B-29/B-30 are parallel (lines 458-465). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:277)_
- `B31-055`: Mandatory inputs include the full SocratiCode indexer/lock/startup patterns, watcher interplay, full ELAI indexing service, multi-session rules, and Phase-1A spec (lines 70-90). _(anchor: DEEP-B-002.discovery.md:278)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0237`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` Checkpoint every fifty files; store blake3 `file_hashes`; skip unchanged content on resume; honor cancellation at every batch boundary. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:411)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0238`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` Metadata schema v2 adds `project_metadata` and `file_hashes`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:412)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0239`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` `IndexLock` uses `fs2` only if already in the workspace, otherwise hand-rolled `O_EXCL+PID`; lock scope is `Index` or `Watch`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:413)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0240`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` PID liveness uses `kill(pid,0)` on POSIX or `OpenProcess` on Windows; stale threshold is two minutes; refresh every thirty seconds; RAII releases on Drop. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:414)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0241`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` `auto_resume_indexed_projects()` is called by both CLI main and dashboard run, starts a fire-and-forget Tokio task per project, resumes `in-progress`, starts a watcher for `completed`, and leaves `cancelled` untouched. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:415)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0242`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` `graceful_shutdown(signal)` has a 60-second deadline, cancels all in-progress work, releases watcher locks, then force-releases lock files after the deadline. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:416)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0243`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` Events: `IndexCheckpoint`, `IndexResume`, `IndexCancel`, `LockAcquired`, `LockContested`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:417)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0244`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` `IndexingProgressPanel` shows live checkpoints and resume/cancel banners. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:418)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0245`: section=`B-31 - Resumable indexing, cross-process lock, and startup auto-resume - PLANNED` Run one-time `migrateAbsolutePathKeys` after B-1 path unification. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:419)_

### B-31.5: Resumable-indexing scale benchmark (release gate)
- **Status:** planned
- **Anchors:** `SUMMARY/SUM-0246` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:425; `SUMMARY/SUM-0247` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:426; `SUMMARY/SUM-0248` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:427; `SUMMARY/SUM-0249` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:428; `SUMMARY/SUM-0250` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:429; `SUMMARY/SUM-0251` @ 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:430; `B315-E01` @ DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:175; `B315-E02` @ DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:176; `B315-E03` @ DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:177; `B315-E04` @ DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:178
- **Composes with:**
  - `B-004/X-E06` ↔ B-25, B-29.5: B-31.5 reuses B-29.5’s exact fixtures, can run in parallel, and explicitly turns B-25’s Empire-corpus claim into a testable scale assertion (B-31.5 L10-L13, L475-L499).
  - `B-004/X-E10` ↔ B-42: B-42 intentionally shares `LockScope::Index` with code indexing. Its dedicated contention test is a Cosmos-worker + code-indexer test, distinct from B-31.5’s two-code-indexer contention benchmark (B-42 L143-L156, L342-L349; B-31.5 L175-L223
  - `B-004/X-E11` ↔ B-33: B-33’s GraphWindow can receive `IndexingComplete`/`SymbolGraphUpdated` updates, while B-31.5 establishes that the indexing/checkpoint lifecycle is stable under crashes and long runs; they are complementary UI versus durability evidence, not
  - `B-004/X-I06` ↔ B-42: B-31.5’s durability benchmark does not prove B-42’s same-lock Cosmos/code coexistence unless the B-42 contention test is also run. The two tests cover different contenders despite sharing the lock primitive.
  - `B-004/X-I10` ↔ B-31, B-42: B-42’s no-rewrite hash behavior and B-31/B-31.5 checkpoint identity establish complementary idempotency layers: artifact-level content idempotency versus batch-level index resume identity.
  - `B-006/INT-013` ↔ B-29, B-29.5, B-31: : B-29.5 is an evidence gate over B-29 traversal/cache claims and depends on B-31 resume; B-31.5 shares fixtures but measures an orthogonal indexing domain (B29.5 lines 1-12, 192-209, 458-461).
  - `B315-E03` → B-29.5: Real fixtures are Linux, Chromium, VS Code, and Postgres, acquired exactly as B-29.5. Referenced SocratiCode coverage is only about 1,000 files, while this plan targets roughly 1,000× larger.
  - `B315-E26` → B-31: Produced scripts/contracts are `clone_scale_fixtures`, `kill_at_progress`, and the three named contracts. They consume B-31 `IndexingService::build_index`, `IndexLock`, `auto_resume_indexed_projects`,
- **Superseded:** `SUP-004` from `B0-063`: B-31’s intended checkpoint/hash/lock/auto-resume/shutdown behavior and five events is listed here as an extension, but the later session status in this same file says it is now DONE. B-31.5 requires clean, kill-resume 10/50/90, lock contention/stale reclaim, shutdown force release, and Chromium memory stability. (B0:L701-712, L157)
- **Superseded:** `SUP-051` from `B315-E15`: After SIGKILL leaves a Postgres lock, the next indexer detects the dead PID, reclaims without manual deletion, resumes, and journals `LockContested` then `LockAcquired`.
- **Superseded:** `SUP-052` from `B315-E24`: `scale_lock_contention_contract.rs` requires instance-B exit <1000ms and stale reclaim <5s.
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0246`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Tier A is full clean indexing of the four fixture scales, estimated at 3-4 hours. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:425)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0247`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Tier B kills and resumes at 10%, 50%, and 90%; SQLite `.dump` must be byte-identical post-resume across at least six runs. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:426)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0248`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Tier C starts two processes; process B exits within one second with `Another process is indexing` plus holder PID; stale PID reclaim completes under five seconds. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:427)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0249`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Tier D verifies SIGTERM shutdown within 60 seconds and forced release after deadline using `ELAI_DEBUG_BATCH_DELAY_SECS`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:428)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0250`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Tier E runs Chromium for four hours and requires RSS plateau/no monotonic leak. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:429)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0251`: section=`B-31.5 - Resumable-indexing scale benchmark - PLANNED release gate` Three CI contract tests are required; their names are not stated here. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:430)_
- `B315-E01`: Prove per-batch checkpointing, `IndexLock`, startup auto-resume, and graceful shutdown on multi-million-LOC repositories under 10/50/90% SIGKILL, concurrent instances, and load; required output is byte-identical non-timestamp state with bounded memory. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:175)_
- `B315-E02`: Scale must exercise 50K files/1000+ SQLite checkpoint writes, the 30-second lock refresh over multi-hour runs, a second process starting 3 seconds later, and a 60-second drain deadline with >60 seconds in flight; this is the evidence gate for a “50M LOC” claim. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:176)_
- `B315-E03`: Real fixtures are Linux, Chromium, VS Code, and Postgres, acquired exactly as B-29.5. Referenced SocratiCode coverage is only about 1,000 files, while this plan targets roughly 1,000× larger. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:177)_
- `B315-E04`: Reuse `scripts/fixtures/clone_scale_fixtures.sh`; pinned revisions are Linux `v6.7`, Chromium `121.0.6167.184`, VS Code `1.94.2`, and Postgres `REL_17_0`. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:178)_
- `B315-E05`: Every fixture’s existing `.elai_cc/` is deleted before a fresh scenario so baselines and kill percentages do not inherit state. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:179)_
- `B315-E06`: Uninterrupted indexing on all four fixtures records total wall time, per-batch mean/p99 (drift indicates leak), peak RSS, total versus checkpointed batches (must match), and final `dkg_nodes` as resume ground truth. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:180)_
- `B315-E07`: Capture each final DKG as gzipped SQLite `.dump` at `metrics/baseline_<tier>.sql.gz` for Tier B identity comparison. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:181)_
- `B315-E08`: `metrics/phase_b31_5_tier_a.toml` records per fixture `wall_secs`, `peak_rss_mb`, `batches_total`, `batches_checkpointed`, `per_batch_p50_ms`, `per_batch_p99_ms`, `final_dkg_nodes`, and `final_chunk_count`. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:182)_
- `B315-E09`: `scripts/scale_test/kill_at_progress.sh <fixture> <kill_at_pct>` starts indexing, polls the latest SSB checkpoint every 5 seconds, computes `batches_processed*100/batches_total`, and sends SIGKILL only once the requested deterministic progress threshold is reached. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:183)_
- `B315-E10`: Kill injection must poll SSB checkpoint rows, never file size or wall clock. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:184)_
- `B315-E11`: Linux and VS Code are mandatory; Chromium and Postgres are nice-to-have. Each fresh fixture is killed at 10%, 50%, and 90%, resumed, then node-count and SQL-dump diffs are persisted, producing six mandatory runs. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:185)_
- `B315-E12`: Diff must be empty except entropy-quarantined `last_indexed_at` timestamps; every non-timestamp column must match byte-for-byte. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:186)_
- `B315-E13`: Tier-B tables record `killed_at_batch`, `resumed_from_batch` (must equal progress at kill), `resume_wall_secs`, positive `files_skipped_on_resume`, `identity_diff_lines=0`, and exactly one `ssb_index_resume_action_count`. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:187)_
- `B315-E14`: Start instance A, wait 3 seconds, then B on the same Linux fixture. B must exit within 1 second; stderr must contain “Another process is already indexing this project, skipping,” holder PID must be logged, and `.elai_cc/.index.lock` must contain A’s PID. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:188)_
- `B315-E15`: After SIGKILL leaves a Postgres lock, the next indexer detects the dead PID, reclaims without manual deletion, resumes, and journals `LockContested` then `LockAcquired`. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:189)_
- `B315-E16`: Tier-C metrics store B exit milliseconds/message/PID evidence plus orphan detection, reclaim time, resume completion, and exactly one contested/acquired action; contract threshold for reclaim is <5 seconds. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:190)_
- `B315-E17`: On Chromium after 60 seconds of work, SIGTERM must finish within 65 seconds (60-second deadline plus 5-second slack), emit `IndexCancel` with nonzero completed batches, and release the lock. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:191)_
- `B315-E18`: `ELAI_DEBUG_BATCH_DELAY_SECS=120` simulates a stuck Postgres batch; SIGTERM after 30 seconds must force release and exit within 65 seconds, logging `force-released at <ts> by pid <X>`. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:192)_
- `B315-E19`: Tier-D metrics distinguish clean and forced cases and record wall time, configured deadline, deadline honored/exceeded, cancel count, lock release, and forced-release log evidence. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:193)_
- `B315-E20`: A complete Chromium ~35M-LOC run records wall time, RSS every 30 minutes, per-batch wall over time, final node/edge counts, and embedding rate-limit evidence including sidecar 429 and OpenAI backoff events. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:194)_
- `B315-E21`: Tier-E metrics include RSS samples, p50/p99 batch time, provider-rate-limit count, final nodes/chunks. Monotonic RSS without a plateau reopens B-31 as a memory leak. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:195)_
- `B315-E22`: `metrics/phase_b31_5_scale.toml` has boolean pass fields for tiers A-E and `overall_pass`; all must be true in the illustrated aggregate. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:196)_
- `B315-E23`: `scale_resume_identity_contract.rs` reads populated Tier-B metrics and asserts zero identity diffs, positive skipped files, and exactly one `IndexResume` per resume; any failure breaks CI. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:197)_
- `B315-E24`: `scale_lock_contention_contract.rs` requires instance-B exit <1000ms and stale reclaim <5s. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:198)_
- `B315-E25`: `scale_long_tail_memory_contract.rs` requires the RSS sequence not to remain monotonically growing—there must be a plateau or drop. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:199)_
- `B315-E26`: Produced scripts/contracts are `clone_scale_fixtures`, `kill_at_progress`, and the three named contracts. They consume B-31 `IndexingService::build_index`, `IndexLock`, `auto_resume_indexed_projects`, `graceful_shutdown`, and `SsbActionType::{IndexCheckpoint, IndexResume, IndexCancel, LockAcquired, LockContested}`; `run_all_tiers.sh` and three cargo-test entry points provide reachability. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:200)_
- `B315-E27`: Contract tests must read populated metrics and may not default-pass when files are missing. End-to-end artifact is `phase_b31_5_scale.toml` with `overall_pass=true` plus green `scale_*` tests. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:201)_
- `B315-E28`: Prescribed sequence is Tier A four clean indexes with per-fixture upper bounds (example Linux <4h on i9+64GB), Tier B six crash runs plus contract, Tier C two scenarios plus contract, Tier D two <65s shutdown paths, Tier E Chromium plus memory contract, then aggregate grep. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:202)_
- `B315-E29`: Done requires clean all-four fixture baselines, baseline SQL artifacts, all six mandatory resume diffs at zero, exactly one `IndexResume` each, and positive hash-skip evidence. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:203)_
- `B315-E30`: Done requires B exit <1s with explicit message/PID, orphan reclaim <5s without deletion, graceful shutdown <65s with cancel/release, and synthetic forced-release log evidence. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:204)_
- `B315-E31`: Chromium must finish, RSS must plateau, per-batch p99 must not progressively exceed 2× baseline, three CI contracts must pass, aggregate must pass, and no dependency may be added without explicit user confirmation. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:205)_
- `B315-E32`: Deliverables enumerate one shared fixture script, kill harness, `run_tier_a` through `run_tier_e`, `run_all_tiers`, three Rust contracts, six TOML artifacts (A-E plus aggregate), and a reproduction README. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:206)_
- `B315-E33`: Create seven scale-test shell scripts, three contracts, six metrics TOMLs, README, and `B-31.5-decisions.md`; modify indexing service only if the debug-delay hook is needed. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:207)_
- `B315-E34`: Decision doc must choose official hardware; decide all-five release gate versus A+B+C minimum. Recommendation is A+B+C minimum, with D+E required for “production-ready”; Linux+VS Code mandatory, Chromium long-tail and Postgres C-focus optional, while Chromium is recommended as an Empire D2 ship blocker. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:208)_
- `B315-E35`: Reaffirm the 60-second shutdown rationale or raise to 120 seconds when cloud embedding batches commonly exceed 60 seconds. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:209)_
- `B315-E36`: Choose byte-exact versus chunk-count-equivalent identity; recommendation is byte-exact non-timestamp data because blake3 file hashes are deterministic. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:210)_
- `B315-E37`: A four-hour non-monotonic RSS check is necessary but insufficient for slow leaks; record the caveat and recommend a nightly long-haul soak. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:211)_
- `B315-E38`: Tier B should not run every commit; nightly is desirable, while pre-release-only is recommended until nightly budget is approved. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:212)_
- `B315-E39`: Benchmark grounds scale claims, detects checkpoint off-by-one/hash/deletion regressions, exit deadlocks, enterprise slow leaks, supplies B-29.5 baseline and B-25 corpus evidence, and establishes scale rerun before merging indexing-service changes. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:213)_
- `B315-E40`: B-29.5 shares fixtures and may run in parallel; after Tier B passes, B-30 should add per-language coverage to the benchmark. _(anchor: DEEP-B-004.discovery.md:214)_

### B-32: Adaptive/minified-file chunking
- **Status:** planned
- **Anchors:** `B005-F07-001` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:316; `B005-F07-002` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:317; `B005-F07-003` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:318; `B005-F07-004` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:319; `B005-F07-005` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:320; `B005-F07-006` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:321; `B005-F07-007` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:322; `B005-F07-008` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:323; `B005-F07-009` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:324; `B005-F07-010` @ DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:325
- **Composes with:**
  - `B-005/X06` ↔ B-1, B-29, B-31, B-33, B-34, B-35: : B-29 requires B-1’s canonical DKG path, integrates B-31 watcher invalidation, backs B-33 symbol visualization, shares cache payloads with B-34, and is parallel-safe with B-32/B-35 (<code>F06:1-10, 206-229, 301-302, 429-448</code>).
  - `B-005/X07` ↔ B-1, B-31, B-35: : B-32 requires B-1, is a natural B-31 batch-refactor sibling, adds minified telemetry to B-31’s progress panel, and supplies B-35’s artifact chunking (<code>F07:1-10, 205-228, 319-337, 373-379</code>).
  - `B-005/X10` ↔ B-29: : B-29 and B-32 both extend the indexing lifecycle but at distinct layers: B-29 builds a symbol graph after DKG construction, while B-32 changes content chunk dispatch before embedding/BM25; both enter full and watcher update paths (<code>F
  - `B-006/INT-014` ↔ B-31, B-35: : B-35 depends on B-31’s lock lifecycle and B-32’s line/character chunkers; the exact artifact lock scope remains open (B35 lines 9-13, 181-185, 210-215).
  - `B005-F07-001` → B-1, B-29, B-30, B-31, B-33, B-34, B-35: — objective/dependencies (lines 1-10): prevent minified, vendored, packed, and generated one-line files from exceeding embedding context or creating useless BM25 chunks by adding deterministic detecti
  - `B005-F07-028` → B-29, B-31, B-35: — downstream (lines 373-379): B-31 shares the refactor, B-29 is independent, and B-35 reuses the chunker for artifacts.
- `B005-F07-001`: — objective/dependencies (lines 1-10): prevent minified, vendored, packed, and generated one-line files from exceeding embedding context or creating useless BM25 chunks by adding deterministic detection and a universal cap; estimate three to five hours, require B-1, bundle naturally with B-31, and allow B-29/B-30/B-31/B-33/B-34/B-35 in parallel. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:316)_
- `B005-F07-002`: — baseline quality (lines 12-18): existing Phase-3K tree-sitter chunking uses function/impl/struct/class regions with fifty-line fallback and reports R@5 0.6333 and R@10 0.7667 on thirty queries. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:317)_
- `B005-F07-003`: — failure chain (lines 20-36): valid one-line JS/CSS/generated code parses as one giant AST region; 50K+ payloads exceed provider context or become noisy, while BM25 accepts semantically poor chunks; current cap occurs only at the provider boundary. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:318)_
- `B005-F07-004`: — pre-strategy detector (lines 38-44): compute average content length per line; a strict value above 500 routes immediately to character chunking and skips AST. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:319)_
- `B005-F07-005`: — universal safety net (lines 46-52): after AST, line-window, or character chunking, enforce <code>MAX_CHUNK_CHARS = 2000</code> before provider pretruncation. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:320)_
- `B005-F07-006`: — safe splitting/identity (lines 54-58): character chunking scans backward for newline, space, tab, semicolon, or comma and identifies chunks by byte offset rather than line number. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:321)_
- `B005-F07-007`: — constants (lines 80-92): define <code>MAX_AVG_LINE_LENGTH: usize = 500</code> and <code>MAX_CHUNK_CHARS: usize = 2000</code>; the latter must stay under embedding tokens times chars-per-token. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:322)_
- `B005-F07-008`: — detector function (lines 94-103): <code>is_minified(content,line_count)</code> returns <code>content.len() &gt; 500</code> when line count is zero, otherwise integer-divided <code>content.len()/line_count &gt; 500</code>; caller supplies the count to avoid allocation. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:323)_
- `B005-F07-009`: — character API (lines 105-114): <code>chunk_by_characters(file_path,relative_path,content,language) -&gt; Vec&lt;FileChunk&gt;</code> owns minified splitting. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:324)_
- `B005-F07-010`: — character algorithm (lines 115-130): iterate byte offset, cap provisional end at 2,000, scan backward to a safe delimiter when not at EOF, slice on a UTF-8 boundary, use <code>chunk_id(relative_path,offset)</code>, calculate start/end-line metadata from newline counts, and advance without overlap. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:325)_
- `B005-F07-011`: — cap behavior (lines 132-147): <code>apply_char_cap</code> mutates every oversized chunk by finding a safe boundary at 2,000 and truncating content there; it is the final operation on every strategy. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:326)_
- `B005-F07-012`: — dispatch order (lines 149-188): split lines/detect language; detect minified before AST; otherwise return capped single chunk for tiny files, capped AST regions when available, or capped line windows as final fallback; emit debug path and computed average on detection. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:327)_
- `B005-F07-013`: — detection event (lines 191-195): emit one <code>MinifiedFileChunked</code> per detected file with <code>{relative_path,char_count,chunk_count,avg_line_length}</code> and surface it under Indexing activity. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:328)_
- `B005-F07-014`: — provider defense/recovery evidence (lines 197-203): retain or add a provider token/character cap; if it fires after universal capping, log “chunk pretrunc fired — should not happen if MAX_CHUNK_CHARS configured correctly” so invariant regression is observable. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:329)_
- `B005-F07-015`: — produced/consumed symbols (lines 205-217): export constants, <code>is_minified</code>, <code>chunk_by_characters</code>, <code>apply_char_cap</code>, and <code>SsbActionType::MinifiedFileChunked</code>; audit <code>chunk_file_content</code>, <code>make_chunks_from_content</code>, and every other knowledge chunk producer for detect-first/cap-last behavior. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:330)_
- `B005-F07-016`: — reachability/stub bans (lines 218-228): execute in full indexing and watcher incremental updates; prohibit any chunk-return path after the cap; reference the max constant at both cap and provider boundary; B-31’s IndexingProgressPanel is the frontend pair. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:331)_
- `B005-F07-017`: — observable contract (lines 229-235): a 50K+ one-line fixture must emit <code>MinifiedFileChunked</code>, create at least twenty-five chunks, and persist no chunk over 2,000. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:332)_
- `B005-F07-018`: — deterministic retrieval checks (lines 237-270): unit tests cover detection/splitting/cap/UTF-8; the fixture creates at least twenty stored chunks; global max length is at most 2,000; search still finds minified substrings; at least one event exists per file; emoji input must not panic. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:333)_
- `B005-F07-019`: — acceptance invariants (lines 273-294): strict 500 threshold, delimiter scan, cap-last on all strategies, byte-offset IDs, UTF-8 safety, per-file event, regression fixtures, provider defense, unchanged-or-improved Phase-3K metrics, and zero non-test unwrap are required. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:334)_
- `B005-F07-020`: — unit test inventory (lines 295-312): named tests cover single-line detection, normal rejection, max size, safe delimiters, unique IDs, truncation, four-byte boundaries, SSB emission, dispatch selection, and cap coverage for giant AST regions. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:335)_
- `B005-F07-021`: — fixture inventory (lines 313-317): add 50K valid JS, 150K valid CSS, generated Go with long strings, and a 50K JS file containing a four-byte emoji every thousand characters. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:336)_
- `B005-F07-022`: — implementation footprint (lines 319-337): add chunking constants/detect/character/cap modules and fixtures; modify BM25 dispatch, knowledge exports, SSB action type, and B-31’s V2 IndexingProgressPanel with optional “Detected N minified files” text; create <code>B-32-decisions.md</code>. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:337)_
- `B005-F07-023`: — threshold/model rationale (lines 339-348): decision record must justify 500 as bundler-sensitive without catching legitimate long lines and verify 2,000 against 8,192-token nomic and 512-token Granite contexts, choosing the conservative smaller-model fit. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:338)_
- `B005-F07-024`: — truncation default (lines 349-351): default to truncating oversize AST/line chunks and discarding their suffix rather than split-and-retain; the latter needs a larger refactor. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:339)_
- `B005-F07-025`: — overlap default (lines 352-355): character chunks are non-overlapping by default because overlap is judged unhelpful for opaque minified content. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:340)_
- `B005-F07-026`: — skip flag default (lines 356-358): record a possible <code>ELAI_SKIP_MINIFIED=1</code>; default is off so vendored bundles remain searchable. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:341)_
- `B005-F07-027`: — side benefits (lines 360-371): bound provider cost/errors, keep vendored/generated code searchable, use an O(n) byte scan without parser allocation, and establish cap-last defense in depth. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:342)_
- `B005-F07-028`: — downstream (lines 373-379): B-31 shares the refactor, B-29 is independent, and B-35 reuses the chunker for artifacts. _(anchor: DEEP-B-005.discovery.md:343)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0252`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` Before selecting an AST strategy, calculate `avg_line_length = len/max(line_count,1)`; over 500 selects character chunks and skips AST parsing. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:436)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0253`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` Apply universal `MAX_CHUNK_CHARS=2000` after every AST, line-window, or character chunker. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:437)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0254`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` `chunk_by_characters` scans backward for newline, space, tab, semicolon, or comma; chunk ID is the byte offset, not a line number. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:438)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0255`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` Boundaries must be UTF-8 safe via `floor_char_boundary`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:439)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0256`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` Emit `MinifiedFileChunked`. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:440)_
- `SUMMARY/SUM-0257`: section=`B-32 - Adaptive/minified-file chunking - PLANNED` Fixtures include 50K-char one-line `bundle.min.js`, 150K-char `bootstrap.min.css`, generated `*.pb.go` with multi-thousand-character strings, and an emoji/UTF-8 variant. _(anchor: 02-B-PLAN-SUMMARY.discovery.md:441)_

  - `B-002/X-017` ↔ B-1, B-2, B-24, B-31, B-39, B-4: Determinism forms a shared boundary: B-1 centralizes paths; B-2 and B-4 use injected clocks; B-24 bans GPU SSIM/LPIPS and selects mode deterministically; B-31 uses blake3/relative IDs/RAII; B-34 uses BTree collections/case-exact rules; B-39
  - `B-005/X06` ↔ B-1, B-29, B-31, B-32, B-33, B-35: : B-29 requires B-1’s canonical DKG path, integrates B-31 watcher invalidation, backs B-33 symbol visualization, shares cache payloads with B-34, and is parallel-safe with B-32/B-35 (<code>F06:1-10, 206-229, 301-302, 429-448</code>).
